# Follow-ups from milestone reviews

Minor findings that a milestone review confirmed but did not fix. Each one names the package
that owns the files involved. That package fixes its items (with a regression test) as part of
its own work and reports it.

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ~~Valley draw calls are far over the §5.4 budget of 250 per view: low preset p50 493 / max 1,084, medium p50 657 / max 1,398 (stress arena p50 860). Batch static props and scenery per district (MultiMesh or merged meshes), and cut or drop shadows at the low preset. P15 must not add unbatched dressing; P17b owns the reduction.~~ **Static world fixed in P17b**: every prop merged into one mesh per material (`SceneKit.merge_meshes`), two shadow splits, no sun shadows at `low`; the static world draws at most 93 calls per view at `low`, 221 at `medium` (regression test in `test_followup_fixes_p17b`). The actors are the rest: see the next row. | P17b (P15: do not make it worse) | `tools/builders/valley.gd`, `data/world/level_valley.json`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` |
| ~~With enemies in view the Valley still passes 250 draw calls: each full-rig enemy costs about 33 (its rig's boxes and its weapon), so five or more in view do (the Valley walk at `low`: p50 256, largest view 656; the stress arena p50 295). Give rigs a visual level of detail by distance: the renderer's `visibility_range_end` on a rig's parts and `visibility_range_begin` on its one-box proxy from about 45 m, with `EnemyActor.set_lod_tier` leaving the proxy to the renderer at tiers 0 and 1 (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "Draw calls after P17b").~~ **Deferred by P18 to the next milestone, documented as a known limitation** (README, `docs/M2_M5_HANDOFF.md`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`). The fix is not small: it changes three actor files, needs a range per archetype (the 8.5 m Ogre King must not turn into a box across its own 80 m arena), and can only be judged rendered; and it would not bring the dense fights under 250 (the courtyard's eight enemies are all within 45 m). A view stays under 250 with up to about four full-rig enemies. **Largely fixed in P22** (decision 9): every rig is one vertex-coloured mesh per animated pivot plus its weapon with one material per actor (the stress arena's twelve combatants 221 -> 77 draw calls, `tests/slow/test_rig_draw_calls.gd`), and a distance level of detail by record (`render_lod_m`: the renderer swaps a rig for its one-box proxy from 40-70 m; none on the Ogre King; `tests/integration/test_render_lod.gd`). The Valley walk at `low`: p50 201, p95 251, max 268 (was 256 / 653 / 656; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`): the largest view of each district is now 238-268, at the budget rather than under it (Thornwick's square with thirteen people within 45 m is the largest); the stress arena's whole view p50 295 -> 227. **Reopened by the M6 gate review**: at the budget is not under it - see "From the M6 gate review (wave 14)" below. | P22 | `scripts/actors/enemy_actor.gd`, `scripts/presentation/actor_rig.gd`, `scripts/presentation/quadruped_rig.gd` |

| ~~Two Valley bot runs on P22's build (intimidate novice seed 3 at the Grove; help_cache novice seed 2 in the village, iteration 1) crashed with SIGSEGV in the audio mixing thread (`AudioDriverDummy`'s thread loop -> `AudioDriver::audio_server_process`; identified from the stripped binary's disassembly; the log's "propagate_notification() ... caller thread" error is the crash handler's own `NOTIFICATION_CRASH` from that thread). Both ran while the container was oversubscribed (load 7-9 on 4 cores); neither reproduced when the same seed ran again; 0 of the 24 runs at P22's base crashed. P22 makes `QuadrupedRig` emit `footfall`, which P19's `CueRouter` already connects to (wolves' paw steps: more voice starts), but a stress-arena soak with six times the wolves' footfall cues (3 x 150 s) did not crash. Find the race in the audio path (the voice pool's `set_stream` on a recycled 3D player while the mixer runs, the exit release, or the engine's mixer) before M6 ships.~~ **Fixed in P23**: the race is Godot 4.6.3's own - a playing player's replaced bus details are freed on the main thread about a frame after the replacement while the mixing thread may still be copying them; `Audio.mix_barrier()` (`AudioServer.lock()` and `unlock()` once a frame) waits out a mix step that is still running, and the music and ambience directors write a volume only when it changes. Reproduced in a stall-injected lab (14 of 17 runs failed, two at the P22 crashes' addresses), 0 of 14 with the fix; 30 audio-on Valley bot runs under CPU load (three at a time beside three busy processes, load average median 11.8 and 16.9 in the two batches, up to 19.9, on 4 cores), 0 crashes; regression test `tests/integration/test_audio_thread_safety.gd` (`docs/systems/P23.md`, "The audio-thread crash"). | The audio owner (P21 in W13, P23) | `scripts/audio/voice_pool.gd`, `scripts/audio/audio_director.gd`, `scripts/audio/cue_router.gd` |

## From the M6 gate review (wave 14)

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The M6 web audio criterion is NOT RUN where it can pass.** Plan §3.5 and the M6 gate ask for no silent run over 50 ms in 60 s of the Hollow's music: that is `node tools/web/audio_check.mjs --strict`. In this container (software WebGL, no GPU) it fails in every run - the gate's runs had 190-191 holes over 50 ms (longest 241-273 ms), every one placed on the main thread drawing a frame of 100-450 ms while the no-threads build's mixer waited (the default form passes those; P23 recorded the same) - and under load (other processes on the 4 cores) the default form fails too (holes the main thread does not explain, a title that took over 600 s, Chromium killed for memory with the forced two-part build). On the gate's final release, alone in the container, two runs: 200 holes (longest 316 ms), all the main thread's (default form OK); and 166 holes (longest 427 ms) of which one, 78 ms, ended 36.7 ms after a burst of driver reads the main thread handled in a 4 ms gap between two long tasks (162 and 89 ms) - 1.7 ms past the placement tolerance - so that run failed the default form. The M6 gate made the check say so: every report carries `criterion` and prints it, a default pass ends "OK (the plan's criterion NOT MET ...)", and a failed run - a timeout included - keeps its trace, the page's state and the console tail. **Run `--strict` on a machine with a GPU and record the result** (or record the owner's acceptance of the default form as a deviation) before M6 is declared passed. **W23 (lane P39f), on its release build: still NOT MET here** - `audio_check.mjs` recorded 61.5 s of the Hollow's music with 68 holes over 50 ms, the longest 3,430.7 ms, every one placed on the main thread (its frames up to 3.5 s with the machine shared; the default form OK; `docs/systems/P39f.md`, "Part web"). Since W23 the default forms of all three web checks also pass a hole the recording's end cut short with no driver read inside it (`holes_cut_at_disarm`, reported apart), an allowance lane P39f added for its own world check's first run; `--strict` is unchanged. **P40 (the release)**: recorded in the handoff's §5.1 (NOT RUN); the release build's `audio_check.mjs` on the quiet machine: OK in its default form, 194 holes, every one on the main thread (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The 0.9.0-m9 release (quiet machine)"). | the owner (deferred by P40: the strict run on a machine with a GPU, plan §6.7; recorded in the handoff's §5.1) | `tools/web/audio_check.mjs`, `docs/systems/P23.md`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` |
| ~~**Valley views at `low` still pass 250 draw calls** (reopens the struck row "With enemies in view the Valley still passes 250 draw calls" above, which P22 left at the budget rather than under it). The rendered Valley walk at `low` (Xvfb 640 x 360, `--walk-teleport`) on the M6 gate's code: max 268, p50 201, p95 251; the largest view per district: Thornwick 268 (its guards and folk, thirteen within 45 m), Barrow 263 (the wardens: the ogre, three wolves and the giant), Quarry 259 (six raiders), Ruins 254 (the courtyard's five), Old Wood 253, Kingsfall 249, Hollow 238 - the same as P22 measured. P22's acceptance ("views with five enemies under 250") and the M6 gate row ("rigs within the draw-call budget with five enemies in view") are **a measured miss by 3-18 draw calls**. Shortening every actor's `render_lod_m` to 0.6 x at `low` (tried at the gate) brings Thornwick to 245, the Old Wood to 248 and the ruins to 244, but not the Quarry (260) or the Barrow (263), whose enemies stand close: those views need cheaper near rigs (for example the weapon merged into its arm's mesh, a merged rest-pose mesh between the near and the box distance, or the crowd renderer's one MultiMesh per pose for humans not striking) or a cheaper HUD. **Partly fixed in P30**: at `low` a rig draws as one merged rest-pose mesh from 0.45 x `render_lod_m` and its full rig again while it acts (`RigPaint`'s mid distance); the rendered walk's largest views are now Thornwick 245, the ruins 246, Hollow 242, the Old Wood 252, Kingsfall 252, the Quarry 257 and the Barrow 266 (max 270 -> 266), and the LOD comparison's fixed view of 25 far members 252-255. The rest needs a cheaper HUD - about 117-120 draw calls of every view at P30's base - and the far proxies batched into one MultiMesh per archetype (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "Draw calls after P30").~~ **Fixed in P27**: the HUD is drawn by `HudKit`'s batching rules (`ui/hud/hud_kit.gd`: every panel, bar and shape as rectangles, shadowed text in four sizes without outlines, no clipping, absolute z layers; `test_hud_layout` checks the rules on the live HUD) and costs about 10 draw calls a view instead of about 120. The rendered walk at `low`: max 154, p50 93, p95 143 (was 266 / 203 / 255); the largest views Barrow 154, Quarry 148, Kingsfall 143, Old Wood 142, Ruins 137, Thornwick 135, Hollow 133, the LOD comparison 143-147 - every view at least 96 under the budget (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "Draw calls after P27"). P39's capture re-measures on real hardware. **P39 (M9)**: real hardware is the owner's (NOT RUN, `docs/HOW_IT_PLAYS.md`); P39's captures measured the world in the container instead - the tour through every region at stage 6 at `low` p50 40 / p95 63 / max 91 draw calls, the formation stress with two engines max 100 (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The M9 performance captures (P39)"). | P30 (its crowd renderer and promotion rules are the plan's tool for many humans in view within the budget; it re-measures the Valley walk at `low`), then P27 (the HUD's draw calls) and P39's performance capture | `tools/builders/{soldier,ranged,npcs,wolf,heavy}.gd`, `scripts/presentation/{actor_rig,quadruped_rig,rig_paint}.gd`, `scripts/ui/hud.gd`, `docs/PERFORMANCE.md` |
| **The M6 gate's Valley matrix misses two items narrowly** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M6 gate"; 24 runs on the gate's code, all complete). The off-camera share of the hits taken over the 24 runs is 20.4 % (target under 20 %; P22 measured 16 %; the novice's runs 16.8 %, the expert's 20.9 %): the runs are new ones (the gate made seeded runs replay exactly, which moved every timeline) and a run's share spreads 4-30 %, while the gate's fixes do not reach the ranged units. And the expert on seed 5 - an extra seed beyond the plan's 1-3 - ran out the forest ogre stage's 240 s watchdog at stage 1 (a death to a hunter's slash, a rest at the Hollow's shrine, the fight again), a forest every policy of that seed plays, before any of the gate's changes act. **P39, iteration 0** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 (P39), iteration 0", tag `p39_iter0_valley`; 24 runs on W22's Valley rules): the off-camera share is 19 % (under 20 %), every novice run and 11 of 12 expert runs complete with no death; the expert force seed 2 ran out the Deadfall stage's 240 s watchdog, and the novice's stage-3 FLEE or ROUTED share is 38 % (M6 gate 52 %, target 50 %; runs 11-61 %). **P39, iterations 1-3**: no change of the three acts in the Valley (the data is the world's bosses, engines and the Matriarch; every bot change acts from stage 4 or in the world only); `force:expert:2` replayed iteration 0's run to the tick (`p39_i3_valley`: 933.33 s simulated, the same flags hash, the same Deadfall watchdog), so the Valley's items stand as iteration 0 measured them. The stage-3 flee share is the confirmation matrix's, on the merged W23 (the other lane changed the raiders' rout from a giant they do not see). **P39, report part**: `docs/HOW_IT_PLAYS.md` ("The Valley") now reports the Valley from that matrix (`p39_iter0_valley`): every novice run and 11 of 12 expert runs complete with no death in the median run, the off-camera share 19 %, the stage-3 flee share 38 %. Open for the confirmation matrix on the merged code. **M9 confirmation (W23, the merged code; `confirm_valley`, 30 runs: `--preset=balance` and the expert on seeds 2-3; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 confirmation")**: the items hold as lane P39's matrix measured them - 17 of its 24 runs replay to the tick, and the other seven part from it where lane P39f's leash return acts (a member sent home rides round a body in its way), with no item moved: the off-camera share 19 %, every novice run complete (18 of 18 with seeds 4-5), the expert 11 of 12 (the force run on seed 2 again out of the Deadfall's watchdog, now with one death on the road back), the stage-3 flee share 38 % on seeds 1-3 (46 % over the 18 novice runs). Open: the stage-3 share and the expert's force seed 2. **M9 gate, the final measurement** (`final_after_valley_a`): the six novice runs of seeds 1-2 read the off-camera share 0.20 and the stage-3 share 0.45; the expert's runs were not in the set (the row "The Valley's items that miss"). | M10 (the shared soldier's awe loss is the lever for the stage-3 share; the expert's force seed 2 was not run at the M9 gate) | `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, `docs/HOW_IT_PLAYS.md` |

| Item | Owner | Notes |

| ~~**The toll fort's tower archers come down from the watchtower and stand behind the closed gate.** Seen while verifying the gate's formation fix (a defied fort, a stage-4 giant on the road 16 m before the pikes): the two archers spawned on the tower (y 25.3) want the giant inside their 12-20 m band (`enemy.archer` tactics), walk down and stop at (464-468, 12, 354) behind the gate, aware and never firing (the palisade and the gate hide the giant). The pikes now carry the fight (the gate's fix), but the tower adds nothing. Give the tower archers a post they hold and shoot from (an elevation marker on the tower, `ai_elevation_points`, and a band that reaches the road, or a hold rule for archers on a tower) when the tower gets its ballista.~~ **Fixed in the M7 polish (lane B)**: the watchtower stood at the fort's back, 54-70 m from the road (beyond the archers' 32 m sight and 30 m arrows); it now stands on the palisade west of the gate, (445, 356), with two elevation markers on its top that the archers spawn on. A ranged unit spawned on an elevation marker its navmesh does not reach holds it as its post (`EnemyActor`, "posts"): the post is its firing point without a search or a path query, its band is its shot's reach (0-30 m), it never kicks, and a flight leaves it there. A defied fort against a stage-4 giant on the road at (455, 376) for 20 s: 8 and 7 arrows, 7 and 7 hits, neither archer left the tower (`tests/slow/test_m7_polish_world.gd`; `docs/systems/P09.md` and `docs/systems/P29.md`, "M7 polish"). P36 still adds the toll fort's stage-5 tower ballista, now on the gate tower. | P36 (adds the toll fort's stage-5 tower ballista; owns its tower content) | `data/world/encounter_crownmarch_toll_fort.json`, `tools/data/world/crownmarch.json`, `scripts/actors/enemy_actor.gd` (P09's markers) |
| ~~**Civilians resolve their safe points outside the path-query budget.** The gate budgeted the enemies' firing-point, flee and rout searches (`docs/systems/P24b.md`, "M7 gate"), but `Civilian.reachable_point` (a closest point and a far path per safe marker tried) still always answers: a town's folk fleeing at once (Brannock's eight villagers when the town turns hostile, Millford's in P37's raid) can take a tick past the 8 queries. Not measured as a hitch yet. Route `_resolve_safe_point` through the same per-candidate budget (`NavQuery.budget_left`, a pending flag the civilian brain waits on), and re-measure the raid.~~ **Fixed in the M7 polish (lane B)**: each safe marker a civilian tries (a closest point and a path) waits for `NavQuery.budget_left` and its own one-marker share of the tick; a search that stops sets `CivilianBrain.nav_search_pending` and resumes the next tick at that marker (the same choice); the agent's path to the marker waits for the budget and is counted. Brannock turning hostile with all eight villagers deciding in one tick: 16 counted queries plus 8 uncounted agent paths in one tick before, at most 8 after, every villager in shelter as before (`tests/slow/test_m7_polish_world.gd`; `docs/systems/P24b.md`, "M7 polish"; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`). P37 re-measures with its Millford raid (raiders in the town besides the folk). **Re-measured in P37**: the raid on the real world (`tests/gate/test_raid_millford.gd`: six villagers fleeing while seven raiders path through the hamlet and burn three houses) spent at most 8 path queries a tick over its 5,910 ticks, 0 ticks over the budget, 209 in all; a villager's search at most 2 a tick; the last villager in shelter after 23 s (`docs/systems/P37.md`, "Measurements"). **P39's capture (M9)**: the world walk through every region at stage 6 (AI on) spent at most 4 path queries in a tick (p95 1) and the novice's nine campaign runs at most 6-8 (p95 1), against the budget's 8 (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The M9 performance captures (P39)"). | P37 (the Millford raid sends villagers to safe markers), then P39's performance capture | `scripts/actors/civilian.gd`, `scripts/ai/civilian_brain.gd` |
| **The M7 world web audio criterion is NOT RUN where it can pass.** Plan §1.3, §6.6 and the M7 gate ask for `stream_check.mjs` on the real world with the audio recorder: no music gap over 50 ms in 60 s across a region border. | **The off-camera threat sting is muted for siege engines and cavalry charges against big giants.** `CueRouter._on_telegraph_started` passes the sting the poise ratio of the attacker's own height (`_height(attacker)`: a ballista 3.2 m, a catapult 4.0 m, a rider 2.6 m), and `AudioMath.sting_suppressed` drops a sting at the 0.25 floor (`skip_at_poise_floor`). The shots strike as an 8 m attacker and the charge as a 4 m one (`attacker_height_override_m`, decision 21), so the sting is muted where the blow is not harmless: a ballista's at stages 5-6, a catapult's at stage 6, a charge's from stage 4. The windup cue and the telegraphs (aim line, landing circle, charge line) still play. Read the action's override in the router (`CombatRules.poise_scale(override if > 0 else _height(attacker), ...)`) and add a cue test for an engine's sting at stage 6. | M10 (the next owner of `scripts/audio/cue_router.gd`; the M9 gate's engine-hit rule left the sting as it was) | `scripts/audio/cue_router.gd` (`_on_telegraph_started`), `tests/integration/test_world_threat_cues.gd` |

Re-measured after it (`docs/systems/P34.md`, "M8 gate"; stage 4, the Reinforced Club, the run's build, the expert's fighting, no assist): without fire her regeneration (25 hp/s, 40 in phase 2) matches what the giant deals (2,605 against 2,602 in 150 s; the drive-out run: 400 s at 0.92-1.00, not driven out). With fire as designed a brazier window costs her 0.16-0.23; the last nest burned with her at 0.589 and she fell to 0.489, but the woodfolk open the wall 20 s after the last nest and she was back at 0.639 by then (not driven out in 300 s). No burning body was ever thrown: a whelp lit in the pit dies of the second stagger it needs to be grabbed again. Put the stage-4 slay and drive-out in P39's balance matrix as a fair fight (fire used as designed, a human-like policy, spacing included: at 7 m the expert parried or evaded 41 of her 49 attacks) and tune in data. Levers: her regeneration at stage 4 (or before the first oath) and in phase 2; `brazier_burn_s` (8) and `brazier_radius_m` (8); `thrown_burn_s` (10) and `thrown_damage` (300); the whelp (health 400, poise 70) or a whelp leaving the pit still staggered; the drive-out's `health_frac` (0.6), or the condition kept once met after the nests burn until the wall opens; the woodfolk's delay (20 s); phase 2 at 50 % (three whelps, 40 hp/s). **P39, iteration 0** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 (P39), iteration 0"): measured at stage 4 with the fire rule (a brazier broken while she stands within 8 m): driven out in one run of two (3 deaths; the other stopped on its sixth death), slain in none (6 deaths, her phase 2 held at 46-53 % by 40 hp/s); slain at stage 5 by the conqueror novices with 6 deaths each, and the experts' 745 s never reached her phase 2. Iteration 1: regeneration 25 / 40 -> 15 / 25 hp/s, the phase-2 summon 3 -> 2, and the bot draws her to a brazier. **P39, iterations 1-3**: regeneration 25 / 40 -> 15 / 25 (iteration 1) -> 12 / 15 hp/s (iteration 3), the phase-2 call 3 -> 2 whelps, and the bot draws her to a brazier (keeping the last one standing in the slay: with the three nests burned the wall opens and she flees, which turned iteration 1's four conqueror slays into drive-outs). Results: driven out at stage 4 (`w_order_wood_novice_1`) in one attempt with no death in the den (iteration 0: six deaths, stopped); slain at stage 5 by the conqueror experts in two runs of three (iteration 0: none reached her phase 2) and by the conqueror novice in one of two (seed 2: six attempts, down to 0.24; her frenzy 3 of its six den deaths). The slay of seed 2 is the confirmation matrix's. **P39, report part** (`p39_rep_world`: the novice's runs again on the final numbers; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 (P39): the report's set"): the drive-out stands (the first attempt, no death in the den, at stage 4 and at stage 5), but neither conqueror novice slays her at stage 5 any more: seed 1, which slew her in one attempt in iteration 2, fails in six since iteration 3's brazier lure (its first lure given up after 20 s, then five of six attempts lost in her phase 2) - with her regeneration lower than in iteration 2, the bot's lure was the cause, not her numbers (confirmed: fix-up 1, below). At stage 4 (`p39_rep_mat4_slay`) the novice does not slay her either: six attempts at 0.45-0.82, three into her phase 2, six deaths (iteration 0 at 25 / 40 hp/s: six deaths at 0.46-0.77). Open for the slay: the drive-out meets the plan at stages 4 and 5, the novice's slay misses at both, the experts slay her at stage 5 in two runs of three. **P39, fix-up 1** (the report's review; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 (P39), fix-up 1"): the review ran the report's code without iteration 3's restart of the lure's walk clock (nothing else changed) and seed 1 slew her on its fifth attempt and completed the campaign, so the lure - this lane's rule of play - had cost the novice that slay; it is now the expert's alone (`docs/systems/P38.md`, "P39 (W23)"). Made again without it (`p39_fx1_world`, `p39_fx1_mat4_slay`), the novice brings her to 0.04 and 0.17 but dies six times on both seeds, every attempt into her phase 2 (her frenzy 5, sweep 4 and stomp 1 of the 12 den deaths); iteration 2's lure did not slay her on seed 2 either, and at stage 4 she never fell below 0.43. Open: the novice's slay is a narrow miss at stage 5 (one run of six slew her) and a clear one at stage 4; her phase 2 (15 hp/s, the frenzy, the whelps) is the lever, with no fourth iteration - for the confirmation matrix and the owner's playtest (`docs/HOW_IT_PLAYS.md`, "The bosses"). **M9 confirmation (W23, the merged code; `confirm_world`)**: the drive-out holds - the first attempt, no death in the den, in all six runs that drive her out (stage 4 in the wood-first order, stage 5 for the peaceful novices and experts); the novice's slay misses on all three seeds (six attempts each, down to 9, 14 and 12 %; her frenzy 7, the whelps 7, her stomp 3 and sweep 1 of the 18 den deaths), and the conqueror experts no longer slay her (none of three: attempts at 37-50 %) on the den that lane P39f's rebuild cleared of 15 trunks ("From the M9 confirmation (W23)", the lure's row). Open, with no tuning in the confirmation. **M9 gate** (lane balance): her phase 2 13 hp/s, one whelp, the frenzy 35 + 40 with a 3.0 s punish window: slain by the conqueror novices with 3, 2 and 1 deaths and by the conqueror experts in two runs of three (at the second attempt and the first; the third drove her out at its first attempt, at 49 %); the drive-out unchanged (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate"). **M9 gate, the final measurement** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate: the final measurement"): the unassisted wood-first order drives her out at stage 4 in two attempts with one death (`final_after_extra_orders/w_order_wood_novice_1`; W23's in one attempt with none), its assisted run at the first attempt with none; at stage 5 the conqueror novice of seed 1 slays her at its fourth attempt with three deaths and the conqueror expert at its first with none (`final_after_world_a`). That still did not hold for the riders whose way home ran through him (the final audit's F11): a stage-6 giant walking east off rider_2's picket after a 10-22 s stand stalled for 30 s with riders 1, 3 and 4 pressed into his shins - their "way" wait (no body on their own post) held exactly where they stopped, against him, for `LEASH_RETRY_S`, and rode into him again. **Fixed at the M9 gate's final round**: a way wait backs off the body in its way on its own side and holds facing it, as a post wait does, and begins at the second path failure (one failed detour) instead of the sixth (`test_war_camp_leash`'s riders' trap: stands of 10, 15 and 18 s, the walk driven by stick input; `docs/systems/P39.md`, "M9 gate (final)"). The camp's leash (70 m, plan §5.10's most) is round its centre (520, 120) and the pickets stand 60-68 m out, so no charge fits inside it at a giant more than about 100 m from the centre, where the capture stood him (40 m east of the pickets). ## From the M8 release check

The release check of the 0.8.0-m8 candidate (its logs: the check's scratch folder
`m8_release/verify/logs/fight_*.log`, its results beside them in `m8_release/verify/results/{rc,pre}_vf_out/`;
"rc" the candidate, "pre" the build before the screenshot pass) found the Iron Regiment's soft-lock (R1, fixed:
`docs/systems/P34.md`, "M8 release") and measured the battle with the novice bot. Its difficulty is left for
P39's balance pass (R3):

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The Iron Regiment cannot be won by the novice bot unassisted at stage 4 or 5, on either build, and the assisted run never reached the Charge.** The full assisted run (`fight_rc_b4a_s1.log`, `fight_pre_b4a_s1.log`: stage 4, level 8, damage taken x0.25 plus assist blows, seed 1) gave up after 4 deaths in 670 s of fighting with the battle's bar at 8,760 / 14,080 (62 %) after every attempt. Of the damage the giant took, the four fort ballistae dealt 5,694 of 6,036 on the candidate and 5,538 of 5,832 on the earlier build (the release check's summary: about 85 % from the ballistae). The bar's 8,760 is the Charge (6 riders, 2,400) and the Last Stand (2,400) to come, the four ballistae (4 x 900) and the Wall's two inner-ballista crews (2 x 180, members of the Wall's encounter at the bailey's far corners): each attempt felled the Wall's 22 infantry and nothing that shoots, so neither of the Charge's rules was met - 60 % of the aggregate (8,448) or every unit of the Wall fallen; one crew or 312 of a ballista's health would have begun it. Measured from the Charge's start (`fight_*_ch4u_s1/s2`, `fight_*_ch5u_s1`: unassisted, 600 s), the riders added little between the builds (rider damage 1,311 -> 1,380, 1,380 -> 1,173, 1,518 -> 1,380: -15 % to +5 %); assisted (`fight_*_ch4a_s1`) Corvane yielded in about 20 s on both. The war camp is somewhat harder at stage 4 on the candidate (`fight_*_camp4u`: rider damage 897 -> 1,311, +46 %; 3,658 -> 4,809 in all; stage 5 unchanged, 1,272). Levers: the fort ballistae's damage or accuracy against a giant (`ability.ballista_bolt`'s power 90 and aim, `prop.siege_ballista`'s 900 health, the engines' cadence); the bot's siege-disruption priority (BotCombat neither breaks engines nor hunts their crews); the assist (`status.bot_assist`); and whether the Wall's phase should wait on its ballista crews and the engines' health (BattleBoss's phase rule, the aggregate). Do not retune before P39 measures a fair fight. **P39, iteration 0** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 (P39), iteration 0"): measured unassisted with each attempt's lowest bar - stage 4 (two runs): 5 deaths each, lowest bars 75-90 % (the Wall not felled), the ballistae 68-77 % of the damage taken; stage 5: 6 deaths, 5 of them to the ballistae, one engaged attempt (84 %). No run reached the Charge, broke an engine or harmed a wall crew (15 ranged tries given up a run at stage 4). Iteration 1: `ballista_bolt` 90 / 140 -> 60 / 100 (power / poise) and the Charge's threshold above the bar the Wall leaves (0.622 on desktop). **P39, iterations 1-3** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`): `ballista_bolt` power 90 -> 60 (its poise stays 140: decision 21, a bolt staggers a stage-4 giant, P32's test). The Ballista Ridge no longer kills the novice (iteration 0: five deaths in a row there), but the battle is not won: at stage 4 `p39_i1d_ir4` 5 deaths (the ballistae 66 % of the damage) and `w_order_crown_novice_1` (iteration 3) six attempts at 0.65-1.0 with five deaths to the fort's ballistae and no Charge; at stage 5 (`p39_i3_ir5`) the Charge began in one attempt of five (at 0.60) and the novice died six times (ballistae 3, the shields' brace 2, a rider 1; the ballistae 74 % of the damage). Left for the confirmation phase: the Charge's threshold (a layout number, so after the merge with the other W23 lane's world rebuild) above both aggregates' post-Wall bars (desktop 0.622, web about 0.67). **P39, report part**: still open for the threshold (`docs/systems/P39.md`, "Decisions and deviations"; `docs/HOW_IT_PLAYS.md`, "Known issues"). The report's crown-first run (`p39_rep_world`) replayed iteration 3's to the tick: six attempts at 0.65-1.0 of the bar, five deaths to the fort's ballistae (76 % of the damage taken in the battle), no Charge. Its CPU per tick p95 in the Aldmere March was 9.3 ms over the battle's 1,054 s, the heaviest place of the novice's runs after the war camp's sabotage (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The M9 performance captures (P39)"): worth a look when the Charge is reached and the riders join. **M9 confirmation (W23): the thresholds fixed as a layout defect** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 confirmation"): plan §5.13 keeps the ballistae shooting into the Charge and P34's phase-spent rule was meant to keep any composition from stalling, so the phases are meant to follow from the fall of the phase before; with the four ballistae (3,600) and the inner pair's two crews counting in the bar, the Wall's fall left 0.622 (desktop) / 0.670 (web) above the Charge's 0.6 and the riders' fall 0.452 / 0.465 above the Last Stand's 0.25. The layout's thresholds are now 0.7 and 0.5 (`tools/data/world/aldmere.json`, chunk c6_3 rebuilt); `test_aldmere_stonehold_content.test_every_battle_phase_begins_once_the_phases_before_it_fall` (every battle, both compositions, stages 4-6) and the gate's `test_iron_regiment_world.test_the_phases_follow_the_wall_and_the_charge_with_the_ballistae_standing` (the real bailey: the Charge at 0.622 / 0.670 and the Last Stand at 0.452 / 0.465 with every ballista and crew standing; 4 failures on the old numbers) guard it. The novice still does not win the battle at stage 4 (`confirm_ir4`: six attempts at 0.78-0.91, the bar never down to 0.70, six deaths) and the crown-first and stage-5 runs stopped on stucks at the fort's gate (the row "From the M9 confirmation"). With the assist (P38's acceptance order, `confirm_world_orders`) the phases now follow in a real run - the Charge at 621 s, the Last Stand at 760 s (bar 0.499) and again at 1,081 s (0.495) - but the giant died in the Last Stand both times (a rider's charge, the east bastion's ballista: the ballistae 58 % and the riders 31 % of the damage taken in the battle) and the crown oath's stage ran out its 1,320 s, so the assisted crown-first order that completed in P38's acceptance (W22) does not complete on the merged code. Kept open for the battle's difficulty at stage 4. **M9 gate (lane aldmere; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate (lane aldmere)")**: the gateway that stopped the crown runs is fixed (the gate garrison's row "From the M9 confirmation") and the battle's composition now follows plan §5.13 - the gate bastions' two ballistae, 16 m up behind a giant at the bailey's gate end, hold their fire until the Charge (BattleBoss; 2 ballistae in the Wall, all 4 from the Charge; `test_iron_regiment_world`). Assisted, the crown-first order completes with no death. Unassisted at stage 4 the novice still loses (`aldmere_e5_ir4`: six deaths, five attempts at 0.97-0.79, the inner pair's bolts five of the five deaths in the bailey; engaged 102 s against 88 s with all four ballistae, `aldmere_fix1_ir4`, one seed: within noise, the M9 gate's final round's correction of "half again as long"), and the cause is no longer the battle: every death reloads the fort gate's waystone, a checkpoint from before the breach (the breach's own request waits on the aware garrison, then on the battle, and a death drops it - the M8 release's rule), so each attempt breaks the gate again for 66-77 s under the garrison's four wall archers and the bastions' two ballistae and enters the bailey with 70-401 of 1,130-1,160 health across the two stage-4 runs (205-401 in `aldmere_e5_ir4`) (the death floor gives back 678); the archers' arrows were the run's largest damage (4,253). At stage 5 (`aldmere_e5_ir5`, the march's help then the breach) the battle reached the Charge in two of its four engaged attempts (0.641, 0.628; with all four ballistae from the first blow every attempt had died in the Wall, `aldmere_fix1_ir5`), the riders ending both, and two more attempts died outside the broken gate: the reload comes back at the fort gate's waystone with the gate broken (the breach's checkpoint committed while the garrison stood down for Brannock's banner), but the breach roused the garrison, 25-30 m from the waystone, and the giant fought it there 130 s each without reaching the gate; six deaths. The battle now tells the giant which ballistae shoot (a HUD line as it engages, another as the Charge frees the bastions' pair; `40afc4e`). Levers for P40: a checkpoint at the breach (or the gate staying broken through a death reload) and, where the reload already keeps the gate broken (stage 5), the roused garrison beside the waystone it gives back; the garrison's fire on a giant breaking the gate; the bot's answer to the wall archers and the bastions' ballistae (it gives up its ranged tries at them, out of sight from its hurl point).  **M9 gate (lane ironreg; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate (lane ironreg)")**: the two levers above are made - the gate garrison falls back to its posts and stands down once the gate is breached, until the giant enters the bailey (`data/world/encounter_aldmere_fort_gate.json`), so the breach's own checkpoint is taken as it falls (an ordinary request: the lane's first answer, the start of an oath's road as a completion request, was taken back at its fix-up 1 - it committed the war camp's first wreck mid-fight) and the fort gate's waystone a death gives back is no fight - and the phases follow the units whatever becomes of the ballistae (the M9 gate review's finding, the row after the crown-first order's). At stage 5 the unassisted novice now wins the battle at its third attempt (`ironreg_after_ir5`: two deaths in it, a rider's charge and the west bastion's ballista; `ironreg_base_ir5` on lane aldmere's code: seven deaths, `bot_failed`). At stage 4 (`ironreg_after_ir4`) every attempt after the first enters the bailey with 641-696 of 1,160 health (165-303 on lane aldmere's code, `ironreg_base_ir4`) and two reach the Charge (0.69, 0.65), but the novice still dies six times, four of them to the inner pair's ballistae (64 % of the damage taken in the battle); in two attempts it stood in the gateway's inner mouth against the Wall's advancing pikes and killed nothing. Levers left: the inner ballistae's numbers (`ability.ballista_bolt`, `prop.siege_ballista`), the bot's play in the battle (its target the far ballistae, past the braced pikes; `scripts/debug/world_bot.gd`) and the assist. Open at stage 4. **The M9 gate's final round and its final measurement** (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate (final)" and "M9 gate: the final measurement"): one attempt on the ballistae's cadence (`prop.siege_ballista`'s reload 6 -> 10 s) still left the stage-4 novice four deaths by 755 s and was reverted; on the final code the unassisted crown-first order (`final_after_extra_orders/w_order_crown_novice_1`, seed 1) loses the battle at stage 4 in five attempts (the Charge twice, at 0.66; the ballistae 65 % of the damage it took there) and the assisted one wins it at the first attempt with no death. Open at stage 4: the final round's row "The Iron Regiment at stage 4". | M10 (balance) | `data/abilities/ballista_bolt.json`, `data/props/siege_ballista.json`, `data/world/encounter_aldmere_iron_regiment_wall.json`, `scripts/debug/bot_combat.gd`, `data/statuses/bot_assist.json`, `scripts/world/battle_boss.gd` |

## From W23 (lane P39f, the web part)

The web world check's runs and the probes beside them (`docs/systems/P39f.md`, "Part web"; the evidence in the lane's
scratch folder `m9_w23/P39f/web/`):

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The worst streaming work frame headless is a props part's text parse, over plan §6.4's 12 ms.** `props_load` is one `ResourceLoader.load` of a chunk's props part; the three largest parts (`c1_2_0` 78 KB, `c2_6_0`, `c4_5_0`) parse in 11.8-12.7 ms from the project's text scenes (median of three, the machine shared), while their instancing (`props_add`, a step of its own) takes at most 2.0 ms and the same parts saved binary - the form exports ship - parse in at most 1.2 ms. Spreading the instancing over more steps cannot help. Under 12 ms headless needs the world builder (parts capped in bytes, which changes the merged meshes and their draw calls, or binary parts in the repository) or threaded loads (which deterministic streaming excludes). **P40 (the release)**: recorded in the handoff's §5.4 streaming row: on the quiet machine the world walk's worst work frame headless is 11.06 ms, a `props_load` (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The 0.9.0-m9 release (quiet machine)"). | M10 (deferred by P40: the world builder's, if it must hold headless; recorded in the handoff's §5.4) | `docs/systems/P39f.md` ("The heaviest streaming step"), `scripts/world/terrain_chunk.gd`, `tools/builders/world.gd`; the probe `props_parts_probe.gd` and its log in the evidence's `probes/` |
| ~~**The world bot's lock invariant fails for one tick when a locked formation's last member routs.** A peaceful world bot run (novice, assisted, seed 1, on this lane's code) ended at 417.4 s on "lock target formation:crownmarch_toll_fort.pikes has no living member": `BotDiagnosis.lock_failure` reads `Formation.living_count()` (routed members do not count) after the Sim's tick, and the player drops a group lock with no living member in his own next `sim_step` (`PlayerTargeting._p30_maintain`, "target_died"), so a last member routing after the player's step leaves the lock held for that tick. The invariant should allow the tick in which the lock's formation lost its last living member (or run after the lock's upkeep).~~ **Fixed at the M9 gate** (lane balance): a group lock whose formation has just lost its last living member passes in that tick and the next and fails if it is still held after that (`BotDiagnosis.lock_failure`; `test_m9_gate_balance`). It ended `gate3_world`'s `w.peaceful:novice:1` at 410 s on the toll fort's pikes; on the fix the same seed completed with one death (`gate4_world`; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate"). | P39 (the bot's invariants) | `scripts/debug/bot_diagnosis.gd` (`lock_failure`), `scripts/actors/player/player_targeting.gd`; the run's log `bot/bot_fixture_peaceful_march.log` |
| **On a slow web client the world's starting ring is not ready in 8 s.** The loading screen loads the ring a 12 ms slice a frame for at most `WORLD_LOAD_LIMIT_S` (8 s) of wall time, then warns ("the world's starting ring was not ready after 8 s; continuing") and lets the giant in while the rest streams. In this container (frames of 0.5-1 s, software WebGL) both of the web world check's loads - the Continue and the fast travel - end on the warning, in every run. By design, and `tools/web/world_check.mjs` lists the warning apart (only `--strict` fails on it); whether a real low-end machine reaches it, and what the giant then sees, is for the owner's real-browser pass. **P40 (the release)**: under a load of 13-15 both of the release build's `world_check.mjs` loads ended on the warning (Enter to control 93.3 s, the fast travel 11.2 s); alone on the quiet machine neither did (13 s and 7.2 s); recorded in the handoff's §5.6 loads row. | the owner (deferred by P40: the real-browser pass, `docs/USABILITY_SCRIPT.md` §11 rows 2-4; recorded in the handoff's §5.6) | `scripts/main.gd` (`hold_until_world_ready`), `tools/web/world_check.mjs` |

## From W23 (lane P39f, fix-up round 1)

Found while confirming the verifier's finding on the target provider's give-up count (`docs/systems/P39f.md`, "Raiders
never fix on a target they cannot strike"; the evidence in the lane's scratch folder `m9_w23/P39f/fixup-1/`):

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Raiders waiting their turn at a house give it up.** The provider's give-up rule (W23) counts only the unit's own damaging blows, and at most two attackers hold a token on one target (`max_attackers`, `Sim.request_token`): in the gate's raid (`test_raid_millford`, the giant standing off) five of the seven raiders round a house strike nothing for 12 s, give it up and leave it for 60 s while the two with tokens burn it - 14 give-ups in a 67 s raid that still burns three houses (the same before and after the fix-up). Whether a turn in the queue should count - a damaging blow by any unit restarting the count, or no count while the unit waits for a token - changes how the raiders spread over the houses and so the raid's pace. | M10 (the raid's pace; the M9 gate did not take it) | `scripts/actors/enemy_actor.gd` (`_p30_futile`, `_p30_on_hit_landed`), `scripts/core/sim.gd` (`request_token`); evidence `fixup-1/logs/raid_probe_*.log` (a print at each give-up, the old and the new rules) |

| **A death in the war camp's sabotage can come back to the camp's fire at the Aldmere road's waystone** (the M9 gate, lane ironreg's fix-up 1, from its verifier's finding): the sabotage's start (`quest.war_engines`, the camp's first engine wrecked, which offends Aldmere) is an ordinary checkpoint request, held while an enemy within 40 m is aware of the giant, and a death drops it - but once the giant is 40 m from every aware member (he steps off the camp, or chases a rider out) it is committed with the wreck and Aldmere at war, and a death in the camp's fight after that reloads the respawn the giant last took. The Aldmere road's waystone (470, 60) stands 18 m from the camp's west catapult and its crew: a probe (`m9_gate/ironreg/fixup-1/probes/test_zz_fix1_camp_later.gd`: the first wreck at stage 4, the giant stepped 80 m off, the request committed at once, then a death) put the giant there with the camp at war, its two ballistae, the east catapult and three riders hit him 12 times in 30 s and a giant standing still was at 64 of 950 health. The same on lane aldmere's head (`5c57636`, the same probe, the same 12 hits): it is older than the M9 gate's lanes; lane ironreg's completion request had made it follow every death after the first wreck, and that is taken back (`tests/gate/test_fort_breach_reload.gd`, the war camp's case). Faction Aldmere has no `respawn_guard_m` (`FactionState.respawn_refusal`, as Brannock's and Millford's 120 m) because the fort gate's waystone, 25-35 m from the gate garrison, would be refused after the breach as well. ## From the M9 gate review (lane review)

The independent review of W22-W23 at the M9 gate (`docs/systems/P39f.md`, "M9 gate (lane review)"; the review's evidence in
the gate's scratch folder `m9_gate/review/`). Its fixed findings are struck in the rows above; this one is recorded:

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Godot's exit-time report after a completed new-game campaign, and after a stopped run that reached Harrok's duel.** Plan §6.2's "0 errors and warnings" passes on the run's own error and warning counter; after the run's `AUTOPILOT_RESULT`, the process of every completed new-game campaign prints Godot's exit report: "ObjectDB instances leaked at exit" (659), 523 resources still in use, 96 Jolt shapes (`P11JoltShape3D` RIDs), 163 meshes, 46 materials, 4 shaders, the `PagedAllocator` lines. Lane P39f's analysis (`docs/systems/P39f.md`, "Godot's exit-time leak report"): every resource still in use is a GDScript (191 of the 249 scripts under `scripts/` and `ui/`) or what the process-lifetime caches hold - `WorldRoot._preloaded` (its 44 entries), `EncounterDirector._scene_cache` (17 enemy and 2 NPC scenes with their meshes, shapes and materials) and the world's two indexes; the leaked instances are those, the scripts' native wrappers, script statics (the formations' registry's WeakRefs, the autopilot's random number generator) and five RefCounted instances. No node, chunk, navmesh or HUD resource; the same fixed set in every run (a loop's second campaign keeps 523), so nothing grows with play and the exit code is 0. **The open question: why some runs print it and others do not** - in lane P39f's runs every completed new-game campaign printed it, and the same completion from a save (`--continue` before the champion or the throne: both clean), its runs that ended otherwise (breaches after 808-1,778 s) and the 330 s Valley loop did not; the final measurement has a stopped run that prints it (below). Clearing the caches before the quit would only hide the report. Not fixed at the M9 gate: no cheap and safe fix was found (each experiment is a whole new-game campaign, about 25 minutes of the shared machine). **M9 gate, the final measurement** (`7502770`; measure-a on `62dcf41` the same): every run that completed a new-game campaign prints the report after its result, as before (524 resources still in use; 559 after the campaign's hand-over from the Valley), and so does one that stopped - the conqueror novice of seed 1, a breach at Harrok's duel after 1,761 s (`final_after_world_a/w_conqueror_novice_1`; `final_world_a`'s on `62dcf41` after 1,595 s). So the report is not only a completed campaign's; the crown-first order's stop at 769 s (`final_after_extra_orders/w_order_crown_novice_1`) and the Valley's runs print none. **P40 (the release)**: recorded, not fixed, in the handoff's §6.2 and §8; no game code changed in the release. | M10 (deferred by P40: an engine report after the result, not a leak during play; recorded in the handoff's §6.2) | `scripts/world/world_root.gd` (`_preloaded`), `scripts/world/encounter_director.gd` (`_scene_cache`), `scripts/debug/autopilot.gd` (the quit after a completed campaign), script statics; evidence: lane P39f's `world/leak/` (three verbose logs and the full run's exit report); the §6.2 rows of `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md` and `docs/HOW_IT_PLAYS.md` |

## From the M9 gate (lane balance)

The M9 gate's balance lane (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate"; `docs/systems/P39.md`, "M9 gate") closed or annotated
the rows above it owns; what its own runs found, and what it leaves:

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A thrown enemy can come down through the ground.** In `gate_world`'s `w.peaceful:novice:1` a shieldman of the Raiders' Hollow, thrown about 30 m up over the stage-4 giant (released in the air above him, 3 m from his centre), fell through the terrain and on for good (y -106 m five seconds later); another shieldman thrown the same way a few seconds earlier landed. Alive and out of every reach, it kept its encounter from clearing and the march's help ran out its watchdog. The lane added the safety net - a living enemy below y -30 m is set back at its home (`EnemyActor.recover_from_void`; the giant has the same rule) - but not the cause: how the body passed the ground (a release spot inside the giant's capsule or the ground, the 30 m floor search of `release_position` from a body 30 m up, or the landing's push) is not found. The probe that showed it: `m9_gate/balance/probe_hollow/` (the bot's trace with the encounter's members). | M10 (the thrown-body release; the safety net is in) | `scripts/actors/enemy_actor.gd` (`release_position`, `floor_below`, `RELEASE_FLOOR_SEARCH_M`), `scripts/world/projectile_system.gd` (`_landing_fallbacks`) |
| **Harrok's slay: the conqueror novice's new stop.** With the Matriarch slain (above) the conqueror novices reach the Moot, and in all three runs of the gate (`gate3_world`, seeds 1-3) Harrok yields (two, two and four times), is struck on, and kills the novice six times: his lowest health per attempt 95/34/17/6/37/40 %, 42/46/9/7/33/35 % and 42/31/17/11/25/34 % (in `gate2_world` seed 2 slew him on the sixth attempt with five deaths); his chop, his frenzy and the Moot's falling stones of his phase 2 are the killing blows. No run of the confirmation reached it (the den stopped every conqueror novice), so it is newly measured, not a regression. His spare and talk duels share his numbers (the novice's median there 2.5 deaths in the confirmation; in the gate's two runs 0 and 2 deaths, in 1 and 3 attempts (w.peaceful:novice:1 talked him down at the first attempt, w.mixed:novice:1 spared him at the third): median 1.0, met at the floor of the range, so easing his shared numbers would take it under 1), so tuning the slay is a change of its own - his phase 2 after a yield struck on (the stones, the frenzy) are the levers - with a den-like harness from a save at the Moot's foot (`m9_gate/balance/scripts/den2.sh` is the den's). The conqueror experts slay him (all three, in two to three attempts with one or two deaths). **Lane balance's fix-up 1** (the verification's major; fixed on the lane's own code): the novice met his fight to the death after the duel with 2-36 % of its health and 0-7 stamina, him at 25-36 %, and lost all of them (a harness from the runs' saves at the Moot's foot, `m9_gate/balance/fixup-1/scripts/harrok.sh`: six deaths on each seed, eleven struck yields lost). His phase 2 is one record shared with the spare and talk duels, so the change is the Strike's: the dialogue's "Strike him down" now lands before he rises - `yield_at.strike_health_frac` (Harrok 0.1: the giant's blow leaves him 10 % of his health; his kit unchanged; Spare and the talk ending never meet it; `test_m9_gate_balance`). The conqueror novices complete on all three seeds, Harrok slain with 3, 2 and 3 deaths (median 3: met; `fx1_world`, the lane's fix-up code; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate", "Harrok's slay"). **Not settled on the final code** (the M9 gate's final measurement; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate: the final measurement"): on the merged code the conqueror novice of seed 1 loses the slay again - on `62dcf41` (measure-a) with two Strikes landed (1,020 damage) and on `7502770` (`final_after_world_a/w_conqueror_novice_1`) with one (510): four deaths before his yield (his chop twice, the Moot's stones, his frenzy), the fifth to his frenzy 3.9 s after the Strike, the sixth to his chop, in six attempts (his lows 32, 38, 40, 27, 6 and 36 %). The merged runs part from lane balance's on the King's Road (the war camp's wall lane ironreg moved) and reach him a level lower (14 against 15); the Strike lands as written. Seeds 2-3 were not run on the final code. Levers, each a change of the slay alone (his spare and talk duels share his phase 2): the Strike's share (`strike_health_frac` 0.1; 0.15 cost more deaths in the lane's harness) and his kit after a struck yield (the frenzy, the chop, the stones); measure from the Moot's foot (`m9_gate/balance/fixup-1/scripts/harrok.sh`) and in full runs on seeds 1-3. | M10 | `data/enemies/harrok.json`, `data/abilities/harrok_*.json`, `data/abilities/moot_stones.json`; `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate" |

## From the M9 gate's final round

| **The Iron Regiment at stage 4: the novice still loses (one tuning attempt missed).** The integration's crown-first runs at stage 4 (`integ_ir4_novice`, `integ_ir4_expert`) lost with six deaths each, the battle's ballistae dealing 67 % and 76 % of the damage. The final round's one attempt, `prop.siege_ballista`'s `engine.reload_s` 6.0 -> 10.0, made the novice's lives 18-56 s longer, but the novice died 4 times by 755 s and the expert 4 times by 747 s (target 1-3), so it was reverted before the assisted order and a stage-5 run were needed (`docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate (final)"). Levers: the inner pair's reach into the bailey's gate end (their 20-140 m band covers the whole bailey); the bot's siege play in the battle (it neither breaks the engines nor hunts their crews, though the HUD says "Smash the ballistae"); a cadence scoped to the battle's engines (a record of their own), measured with the assisted order and a stage-5 run; the death reload's health (678). **M9 gate, the final measurement** (`final_after_extra_orders/w_order_crown_novice_1`, seed 1, unassisted): the battle lost at stage 4 in five attempts - the Charge twice, at 0.66; in two the Wall's pikes killed the giant in the gateway's inner mouth before the bar moved - five deaths in it (the pikes 3, the north-west ballista, a rider), the ballistae 65 % of the damage it took there; the bot broke no engine and gave up its ranged blows at the west bastion's ballista twice. The levers stand. | M10 | `data/props/siege_ballista.json`, `data/abilities/ballista_bolt.json`, `data/world/encounter_aldmere_iron_regiment_wall.json`, `scripts/debug/bot_combat.gd` |
| **The Matriarch's phase 2 is lighter than her phase 1 by her own blows.** Her own phase-2 sequence (frenzy 35 + 40, stomp, whelp throw, frenzy, sweep) brings 0.74x her phase 1's power per second (18.3 against 24.6; 1.18x on the confirmation's numbers): the frenzy's sweeps are among her lightest blows and the second ends in the 3.0 s recovery, the longest window of her kit; regeneration is 13 hp/s against 12. The phase's 1.7x comes from the one whelp her roar calls, which the gate suite models as swiping throughout (22.6 power per s, 55 % of the figure) - once it is killed or thrown she hits lighter and less often than in phase 1. The kill target (1-3 deaths) is met (3, 2 and 1). Whether her phase 2 should still feel like the escalation without its whelp is the owner's playtest question (HOW_IT_PLAYS). Levers: the frenzy's power with its 3.0 s window kept; the phase-2 sequence (without the trailing sweep or the second frenzy); the summon. Re-measure any change with `m9_gate/balance/scripts/den2.sh` on seeds 1-3 from the level-10 den saves, against 1-3 deaths, the drive-out and the experts unchanged; the gate test guards her own rate at 0.7x or more. | M10 (after the owner's playtest) | `data/enemies/troll_matriarch.json`, `data/abilities/matriarch_frenzy_*.json`; `tests/gate/test_matriarch_phase_two.gd` |

## From the M9 gate's final measurement

round"). What it found or left:

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The world matrix on seeds 2-3 on the final code was not run.** The final measurement, like measure-a before it, ran the novice and the expert on the three presets at seed 1 only (the owner asked for speed), though plan §6.4 keeps the expert on seeds 1-3 when wall time is short. So the release's world verdicts are seed-1 verdicts: the novice's "at least 2 of 3 seeds per preset" is open (peaceful 1 of 1, conqueror 0 of 1 - lost at Harrok's slay - mixed 1 of 1) and the expert's "3 of 3, at most 2 deaths each" met on seed 1 (0, 1 and 2 deaths). W23's matrix (`a7da72d`, `confirm_world`) is the last one on three seeds; lane balance ran seeds 2-3 of the conqueror and of the peaceful experts on its own code only. Run `--preset=world_balance`'s seeds 2-3 (twelve runs; the seed-1 batch's six took 15-26 minutes of wall each, three at a time) with `--extra=--perf` on the release code and record them beside the seed-1 runs. **P40 (the release)**: the handoff's §5.4 gives the world verdicts as seed-1 verdicts; the release ran no further matrix. | M10 (P40 recorded the release's verdicts as seed-1 verdicts in the handoff's §5.4) | `tools/run_bots.sh` (`--preset=world_balance`), `tools/bot_report.py` (`world_targets`); `docs/BALANCE_LOG.md`, "M9 gate: the final measurement" |
| **The war camp's south edge holds the giant in the crown's sabotage** (new on the final code). `final_after_world_a/w_mixed_novice_1`'s sabotage took 783 s against 546 s on `62dcf41`, with three stuck moments at the camp's south edge on the crown's oath - (540.8, 74.3) a sidestep and jump, (516.4, 65.3) a replan, (513.3, 57.5) a far replan - where its measured run had none; `w_mixed_expert_1` met the same edge once (536.6, 75.8), and the mixed assisted run's sabotage took about 180 s longer too (the oaths stage ended at tick 43,970 against 33,161). The camp's centre is (520, 120) and its leash 70 m, so the edge is about 50-60 m south of it. The runs part from their measured runs earlier (the firing-point rule, `6bdf7e5`), and the stucks were traced no further than that parting: find what holds the giant there (the camp's members on their leash, a picket's rider, the ground) with `--bot-trace=1` on `w.mixed:novice:1`, and whether a player would meet it. | M10 | the runs `user://bot_runs/final_after_world_a/` and `final_after_assisted/`; `data/world/encounter_aldmere_war_camp.json`, `scripts/debug/world_bot.gd` (the stuck recoveries); `m9_gate/final/measure-after/reports/cmp_runs.txt` |

## From P41 (W25: the giant's asset pipeline)

The asset pipeline and the model (`docs/systems/P41.md`; the measurements in `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The giant's
assets (P41)"). What it leaves for the later packages:

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The owner's 26 MiB answer (§0.6, answer 1) is not applied yet.** W25 keeps the plan's default: the Web presets exclude `assets/giant/tex_hi/*` (and the manifest) and `build_web.sh`'s budget after M7 stays 23 MB. Applying the answer is three edits: the budget line (`else BUDGET_MB=23` to 26) with `test_build_web_budget`'s expected value, `assets/giant/tex_hi/*` out of both Web presets' `exclude_filter` with P41's test in `test_web_presets` (`test_the_web_presets_leave_out_the_giant_manifest_and_the_hi_tier`, which asserts the exclusion) turned around, and GiantLook's tier choice on the Web. `GIANT_SHARE`'s cap (the budget less 18.4 MB) and `build_giant.py --budget` (it reads the preset and build_web.sh) follow by themselves: 7.6 MB. Measured: the two maps add 1,814,464 bytes shipped (1.73 MiB, not the plan's 1.3), so the floor's share would be about 4.37 MiB and the pack about 21.1 MiB. | P45 | `tools/build_web.sh`, `export_presets.cfg`, `tests/integration/test_web_presets.gd`, `tests/integration/test_build_web_budget.gd` |
| **The fullest look's primitives beyond 120 m at 1080p.** Counted from the imported LODs (stage 1, 75° field of view): 2,078 triangles at 1280 px wide but 4,802 at 1920 px until about 151 m, because `Hair_long_back` has only two LODs (2,700 and 702 triangles; Godot's simplifier stops early on cards). The budget is ≤ 4,000. If P45's probe confirms it: a coarser card LOD of its own in `kit/giant_hair.py` (fewer, wider cards), a `visibility_range` hand-over to a reduced card set, or the hair's `lod_bias` from GiantLook. | P45 (probe), then P46 (hair) | `tools/giant/kit/giant_hair.py`; `docs/PERFORMANCE.md`, "The giant's assets (P41)" |
| **`scar_brow` is small**: 30 texels at 1024 px and 8 at 512 (the floor's other scars 30-139 at 512). The creator brief's rule (strokes of at least 4 cm at stage 1) is met by the bold designs; the brow scar is the exception. P44's option check at the Face framing decides; if it does not read, widen it in `kit/designs.py` (`d_scar_brow`; its segments come from `giant_skin.scar_segments`) and regenerate (`--designs`; the body is not touched). | P44 | `tools/giant/kit/designs.py` |
| **The tangent-space normal bake jitters by a texel.** Baked again on the same inputs and machine, `normal.png` came out one texel different (one channel by 1/255, of 1,048,576) in 3 of 14 bakes with the micro-relief tile made beforehand and in 3 of 4 with the tile made inside the bake's process; every other pass was the same each time. `--verify` now tolerates at most 64 texels off by 1/255 in a Cycles map on the reference toolchain (`TOLERATED`), and `build_giant.py` makes the tile in a separate interpreter. The cause inside Blender was not traced (most likely a tie between two high-poly faces in the selected-to-active ray cast: the texel lies inside a hand island). Tracing it, or baking that pass single-threaded if that makes it deterministic, would let the verify be strictly bit-exact again. | M10 (P46 or the gate) | `tools/giant/kit/bake.py`, `tools/giant/manifest.py` (`image_jitter`) |
| **`--verify`'s tolerance path is untried on another toolchain.** This container is the reference toolchain, so every regeneration here is bit-exact; the `TOLERATED` comparison (Cycles pixels within 2/255, 0.1/255 on average) runs only on synthetic images in the pipeline's tests (`ToleranceTest`). The first run on a different CPU or wheel set (the owner's machine) shows whether the tolerances hold. | M10 (owner) | `tools/giant/manifest.py` (`image_close`), `tools/giant/build_giant.py` (`verify`) |

| **First person looking down shows the open tops of the braies' limb parts.** Layer 2 (the torso and the garments' core parts) is culled in first person; the limb part of the braies has no cap where it meets the core, so two grey rings show above the thighs at pitch -60° or lower (the gate's frames `s*_fp_down.png`). | P46 (garments), gate frame review | `tools/giant/kit/giant_garment.py`: cap the limb part's top loop, or move its top band into the core part; regenerate with `build_giant.py` and re-run `tests/slow/test_skinned_rig_render.gd`. |
| **The hand clips carry a track for every bone.** `clip_bones` keys only a hand's fingers in `grip_r`, `grip_l`, `open_r` and `open_l`, but the glTF export samples every bone of every clip (`export_force_sampling`, no animation-size optimisation), so each imports with a track per bone though only the fingers pass their blend filters; `grip_r` is evaluated every frame. Exporting the hand clips without the unkeyed bones (or dropping those tracks at import) cuts the tree's work by about a third of a clip a frame. | P45 (if the Web bench asks) | `tools/giant/kit/clips.py` (the export call); `SkinnedRig._finger_paths` already filters the blends. | **P43's two rows for P45 are answered.** The hand clips' unkeyed tracks: the Web bench does not ask for the change (0.116-0.128 ms a frame against 0.35). | **The Web build's page grows without bound when the browser is starved of CPU** (gate finding LD-1). With the whole browser on one CPU beside busy loops, or on a machine at loads past 10 with two lanes' renders, the page's renderer process gains 16-58 MB a second while a level loads (New Game's Valley, a Continue) and never stops; the kernel's OOM killer then takes the browser and whatever shares the machine. The 0.9.0-m9 build does the same pinned to one CPU (16 MB/s to the 2.5 GB watchdog), so it is not the realistic giant's; with evenly competing load (10 and 24 busy loops, loads 10-30) the M10 build's Valley loads 43-50 s after Begin and settles at 1.26-1.5 GB. It is anonymous memory of the renderer process only (not the GPU process, not shared memory, not the JS heap). The cause inside the page was not isolated. | M11 (the Web build), with a browser that has a GPU | The five Chromium checks now stop at 3 GB with a FAILED line naming the memory (`tools/web/memory_guard.mjs`, `GIANT_WEB_GUARD_MB`). To isolate it: the same starved run with `--no-skin`, with `--no-audio`, and in a browser with a GPU; the suspects are the single-threaded build's frame queue under software WebGL (frames submitted faster than SwiftShader draws them) and the audio worklet's buffers ("SyncReader::Read timed out" in Chromium's log). What M10 adds under the same moderate load is 0.2-0.5 GB after New Game (1.26-1.5 GB against 1.0-1.1 GB). |

## From the 0.10.0-m10 release

What the release step found when it built the Web release again after the owner's approval (`docs/PERFORMANCE.md`,
"The 0.10.0-m10 release, rebuilt after the owner's approval"). The two Web tools' rows the release added are the last
two of the table above.

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`tools/web/world_check.mjs`'s default form fails on a step or a drift of the audio clock.** The recorder (`placeHoles`, `tools/web/audio_recorder.mjs`) maps the audio clock onto the page's clock with one straight line between a reference at arm and one at disarm, and places a hole on the main thread only when a driver read ends it within 35 ms. Over the check's 370-405 s of music in headless Chromium here, the audio context's clock sometimes falls behind in one step (31-194 ms in the failed runs) or drifts unevenly; the holes on one side then sit that far from their reads and the check ends "N hole(s) over 50 ms not explained by the main thread", although the same rule places every hole (but the first two or three at a step) once each hole is moved by the offset of the holes around it. On the release day: 6 runs of 6 failed with the first rebuild's pack, and 1 of 5 with the checked build's (by 10 ms, with no step); the checked build's busy run earlier that day failed the same way. `audio_check.mjs` (60 s) and `stream_check.mjs --audio` (135 s) share the mapping and stayed inside the tolerance. | M11 (the Web tools) | Map the clocks piecewise (a reference every few seconds, or the offset followed from the holes' own reads) and report the largest step it saw; a case in `tools/web/test_world_check.mjs` and `test_audio_check.mjs` for a clock that steps and one that drifts. `--strict` (no hole at all) is unchanged. |
| **A Web export is not the same bytes in two checkouts, and one such difference went with a failing check that is not explained.** The scene files carry no node ids (the builders strip them), so the engine gives each node a random id when it converts a text scene for export, and each checkout keeps its conversions in `.godot/exported/` (not in git). The main checkout's fresh conversion of `scenes/player/giant_model.tscn` gave a pack 23 bytes different from the checked build's (two node ids and that file's MD5). The release was therefore built with the checked build's conversion of that one scene copied into the export cache, which reproduces the checked build byte for byte. Not explained: with the first rebuild's pack `world_check.mjs` showed an audio-clock step of 31 ms or more in 6 runs of 6, with the checked build's pack none over 27 ms in 5 runs of 5, the same machine and hour; the engine's source reads those ids only to pack a scene or to recover a moved node, and every other packed byte is the same. | M11 (the build tools; the Web tools for the audio part) | Make the export reproducible: keep stable ids in the scenes the builders write (a seeded id per node path instead of stripping them), or have `tools/build_web.sh` export from an empty `.godot/exported/` and record the pack's checksum beside `PCK_SIZE`; a test that two exports of one commit give the same pack. For the audio part: `world_check.mjs` on two packs that differ only in those ids, several runs each, in a browser with a GPU; the first rebuild's files are in the ship step's `w28/web_trial/` folder and the reports in `w28/release/checks/`. |

| Item | Owner | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **`GiantLook` does not call `ensure_part` or `release_unused` yet.** W29 keeps `giant_look.gd` as it is: `GiantModel` holds an empty node for every part, loads the part when the node is shown and lets it go when it is hidden, so a look's parts are the loaded ones without `GiantLook` knowing. | P51 (W30, `giant_look.gd`'s owner) | `GiantLook.apply` can call `model.ensure_part(id)` before it shows a mesh and `model.release_unused(shown)` after; the creator's preview that should keep the open category's parts resident calls `ensure_part` for each (a hidden node with a loaded mesh stays loaded until `release_unused` or the next hide). The recent list (`PART_KEEP` 6 parts, `PART_KEEP_BYTES` 1 MB) already spares the reload of a style turned off and on. |
| **The index names a part's file `part_path`, not `part`.** `part` is M10's key for a garment's body part (`torso` or `limbs`), which `test_skinned_rig.gd` and `test_giant_assets.gd` read. | P53 (the index v2) | Either keep `part_path` in §2.6's index v2, or rename M10's `part` to `body_part` there with the tests that read it. |
| **The shadow proxy needs the build sliders' vertex push.** The proxy carries the build weights in `COLOR.g` and `.b` like the skin, but draws with the glTF's placeholder material (it is drawn in shadow passes only, so nothing else matters today). With a build below zero the skin moves inside the proxy, which then shades it. | P53 (the uniforms `build`, `build_max_m`) | Give `GiantModel.shadow_proxy()` a material whose vertex function pushes like the skin's (the scratch probe's four-line shader did it; the shadow pass follows a vertex shader, probe 2). |
| **The build weights leave the head alone.** The weights fade with the head's own skin weight, so the face, the eyes, the hair roots and the beard roots do not move with the build sliders (the neck does). Hair and beard cards still carry the channels (the neutral 0.5 on most of them). | P53, P56 | If the neck's change under a long beard shows a gap, the beard's shader can push with its own channels: they hold the weights of the skin each card grew from. |
| **The brawn and girth directions come from four more vendored CC0 targets.** `universal-male-{young,old}-averagemuscle-maxweight` and `-maxmuscle-averageweight`, from the same audited wheel (`tools/giant/mpfb2/`, 137 files now; 181,311 bytes more). `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §23 still says 133 files. | P52 (the ship package edits `ARCHITECTURE.md`) | The vendored targets held only the giant's own corner (both sliders at their most), which gives one direction, not two. Question 6 of the plan (more CC0 targets) was about women for the people; these four are the giant's own macro family. |
| **The cape is a single sheet with a rest shape; nothing moves it yet.** It is weighted to `cape_c`, `cape_l`, `cape_r` below the shoulder blades and to the body above; it still `covers` the lower back (region 6), so the outfits' data and the designs' rule hold. Until `SecondaryMotion` runs it hangs like a board from the shoulders in a run or a roll. | P50 (`SecondaryMotion`), P51 (cloth) | The chains' starting values are in the index (`chains[*].stiffness`, `drag`, `gravity`, `max_deg`); they are guesses, not tuned. The collar band round the neck's base sits under the long beards. |
| **`Hair_long_back`, `Hair_warrior_tail` and `Hair_wild_mane` hang on the back chain only.** Their side locks reach the side chains by less than half, and a chain no vertex hangs on by half is folded back into `head` (so the crop and the short beard need no springs). | P51 (the hair), P56 (braids) | `giant_parts.CHAIN_LEAST`, `ARC_HOLD`, `ARC_FREE` and `SIDE_PHI` are the dials; longer side locks or a lower `CHAIN_LEAST` bring the side chains in. |
| **The sprint keeps the brain's 3.2 m a cycle.** "A longer stride" is a longer flight: the clip is seeked on the brain's walk phase like the walk and the run, so a longer ground stride would slide. | P50 | If the brain ever lengthens its stride with speed, `clips.GAITS["sprint"]` takes a cycle of its own (`CYCLE_M` is one constant today). |
| **The turn clips pivot about the model's origin**, 90 degrees in two steps on a clock of the yaw travelled, the inside foot first; a standing foot stays where it stands while the body turns over it, and the clip ends as it starts, so a longer turn can wrap. | P50 | A turn of less than 90 degrees stops with one foot turned out (the clip at that fraction); the rig's cross-fade back to the stance covers it. |
| **`carry_two_hand`, `fp_rest`, the six grips and the four flinches were judged from two cameras each, not in play.** They are starting poses for P50's frame review (the flinches are offsets from the rest pose on the spine, neck, head and clavicles; the grips key both hands' fingers). | P50, P51 (the next asset writer regenerates) | `tools/giant/kit/clips.py`: `GRIPS`, `FLINCH`, `carry_pose`, `fp_rest_pose`. `tests/support/giant_parts_probe.tscn -- --review` draws them. |
| **`ArrayMesh.shadow_mesh` is empty on every skinned mesh of the giant, the body's included**, with `meshes/create_shadow_meshes` on (read in the scratch probe and in the game's import). The parts are imported with it off; nothing was lost. | P49, P57 (the shadow passes' cost) | The importer makes no shadow mesh for these surfaces in this engine version; the shadow proxy is what makes a shadow pass cheap. |
| **A part ships about 13.5 KB of skeleton and skin of its own** (71 bones and 71 binds in every part's imported scene; 16.9 KB by the fit over triangles). 21 parts: about 0.28 MiB of the giant's 6.32 MiB share, where the plan allowed 0.10 for the proxy, the cape and the parts' overhead together. The 34 more parts of Part B would add about 0.45 MiB that the plan's per-triangle arithmetic does not count. | P51 (W30, the next asset writer) or P54 (before the 25 garments) | Export each part with the bones its mesh is weighted to only (mark the others non-deforming and export deform bones only, or rewrite the glTF's skin as `clips.strip_tracks` rewrites tracks). The part then keeps a skin of its own, bound by name (probe 1 showed that works; `GiantModel.ensure_part` already falls back to the part's skin when it differs from the model's, `same_binds`). To weigh against it: one `Skin` for every mesh is one set of bone matrices a frame; a skin a part is a few more small ones. |
| **`tools/giant/test_giant_tools.py` reads `tools/build_web.sh`'s last `BUDGET_MB=` as the Web budget (26).** P49 teaches the script the budgets of m11 to m13 in the same wave. | The W29 merge | If the merged script's last assignment is no longer 26, `BudgetTest` and `build_giant.web_budget_mib()` need the milestone's rule (the estimate is judged against the budget less 18.4). |

| **The thrown soldier's get-up is the fall clip played back** (within the 0.6 s release stagger: about 0.2 s down, 0.12 s lying, 0.27 s up); it reads at the game camera as a knockdown and a quick recovery, and as a rewind at 3 m. A get-up clip would fix it. | the asset lane | `PersonRig.knockdown_time`. |
| **The chop's and the brace's legs are the clips' stance**: the giant chops and the pikeman braces with the stance's legs (only the spine bends); a knee bend at the chop's impact and a deeper brace would sell the weight. | a motion pass | `PoseBrainModifier.CHOP_KEYS`, `PersonRig.STRIKE_KEYS`. |

| **Women's faces**: the women share the men's face texture with reweighted targets; their own face texture and softer hairline notches would read better up close. The owner accepted 0.13.0 shipping without it. | a people asset pass | `tools/giant/kit/people_*.py`; `docs/systems/P65c.md`. |
| **The wolf's ears are bare cones** (the fur stops at their base). Accepted for 0.13.0 by the owner. | a creature asset pass | `tools/giant/kit/quadruped.py`. |
| **The horse has no saddle** (the rider sits on the bare back). Accepted for 0.13.0 by the owner. | a creature asset pass | `tools/giant/kit/quadruped.py`, the rider's seat in `assets/creatures/manifest.json`. |
| **The troll's and the ogre's heads show facets** at the brow and jaw at close range. | a creature asset pass | `tools/giant/kit/brute.py`. |
| **The brutes' sweep has no grip for the off hand** on a two-handed club (it follows the haft, open). | a creature pass | `scripts/presentation/brute_rig.gd` (`SWEEP_KEYS`). |
| **The horse's gallop is procedural** (a keyed flexion of the legs, not an authored clip); an authored gallop clip would carry more weight. | a creature asset pass | `scripts/presentation/skinned_quadruped_rig.gd`. |
| **A kilt at a heavy build can let the thigh through**: the kilt's hanging skirt takes the hip's build weights while the thigh's are larger, so a giant with high brawn and girth (the player's included) can push a thigh through the cloth. Harrok's preset was trimmed to clear it. | a giant asset pass | `tools/giant/export_game.py` (give the skirt the build weights of the skin it hangs over); `docs/systems/P65d.md`. |
| **The Web check never times the first brute, boss or rider**: `PeopleWebProbe` times the first formation and the first wolf only. Measured by hand at Gate C: 1.79x, 1.59x and 2.16x of the median frame (budget 3x). | a tools pass | `scripts/debug/people_web_probe.gd` (add `brutes`, `bosses` and `cavalry` views), `tools/web/people_check.mjs`. |
| **The giants' axes are built in code** (`WeaponVisuals._axe`) and read as props beside the new bodies. | a weapons look pass | `scripts/presentation/weapon_visuals.gd`. |
| **The Web checks' memory guard trips on a loaded machine**: with three other jobs running, the open-world start, audio and world checks stopped at about 3.1 GB of renderer memory on 0.13 and on 0.12 alike; on a quiet machine the 0.13 open-world start peaks at 1158 MB (0.12: 1130 MB). Run the Web checks alone. | whoever runs the checks | `tools/web/memory_guard.mjs`. |
